I’ve actually missed a few of my own doctor visits. And I tangled up the schedule at the senior center so badly that my husband has had to step in and help. It's just getting too hard to keep track of it all without his help.\n\nDoctor: Have these memory and thinking problems been steadily getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's steadily getting worse, to be honest. I just know that right now, it's a real problem and it's very frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor told you that these memory problems are due to a medical condition affecting your brain or body?\n\nPatient: No, no doctor has ever said anything like that to me. I haven't been told that it's from a medical condition or anything wrong with my brain or body. I just know my memory isn't what it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling very anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get frustrated when I forget things, but I wouldn't say I've been feeling overly anxious or worried about everything. Just... normal, I suppose, other than the memory stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gotten lost in a place that used to be familiar to you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I might forget where I put my keys, but I always know where I am. I haven't ever gotten lost in my own neighborhood or anywhere I know well.\n\nDoctor: Has any doctor ever told you that a medical illness or brain condition is causing your memory problems?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me that one a minute ago. But no... like I said, no doctor has ever told me it was from a medical illness or a brain condition. Nobody has ever said anything like that to me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you noticed problems with your thinking or judgment, not just your memory?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My family hasn't said anything about my judgment or my thinking. I just know that right now, it's a real problem and it's very frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor told you that these memory problems are due to a medical condition affecting your brain or body?\n\nPatient: No, no doctor has ever said anything like that to me. I haven't been told that it's from a medical condition or anything wrong with my brain or body. I just know my memory isn't what it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling very anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get frustrated when I forget things, but I wouldn't say I've been feeling overly anxious or worried about everything. Just... normal, I suppose, other than the memory stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gotten lost in a place that used to be familiar to you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I might forget where I put my keys, but I always know where I am. I haven't ever gotten lost in my own neighborhood or anywhere I know well.\n\nDoctor: Has any doctor ever told you that a medical illness or brain condition is causing your memory problems?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me that one a minute ago. But no... like I said, no doctor has ever told me it was from a medical illness or a brain condition. Nobody has ever said anything like that to me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you noticed problems with your thinking or judgment, not just your memory?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My family hasn't said anything about my judgment or my thinking. It's really just the memory things that come up.\n\nDoctor: Compared with how you used to be, has your memory clearly gotten worse?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say it's clearly gotten worse. I mean, I'm getting older, so I might have those little moments like with the keys, but... no, there hasn't been any big change or anything like that. It seems about the same to me.\n\nDoctor: Have your memory problems caused you to stop doing important activities or responsibilities you used to handle on your own?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I guess they have. I’ve missed some doctor visits recently, and I tangled up the schedule at the senior center so badly that my husband had to step in. He’s been helping me with a lot of it now—making the phone calls, keeping the calendars straight, and even handling repair decisions around the house that I always used to just take care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Has any doctor ever diagnosed you with dementia or a neurocognitive disorder?\n\nPatient: No, no doctor has ever told me I have dementia. Or... whatever that other word was you used. I've never been diagnosed with anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you noticed that you repeat the same questions or stories because you forget you already said them?\n\nPatient: No, my husband hasn't mentioned anything like that. We talk quite a bit, and I don't think I'm repeating myself or asking the same things over and over. If I was, I'm sure he'd let me know.\n\nDoctor: Has your husband noticed that your memory has been getting worse?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, he stepped in to help with the calendar and the phone calls, like I mentioned, but he hasn't actually said anything about my memory getting worse. If he's noticed something like that, he hasn't mentioned it to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any memory or thinking tests done by a doctor that showed a problem?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any tests like that done. My regular doctor has never asked me to take a memory test or anything, so there hasn't been anything to show a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a memory test or thinking test that showed your memory was actually impaired?\n\nPatient: Like I just mentioned a moment ago, no. I've never taken any kind of memory test, so there hasn't been anything to show it's impaired.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's just getting too hard to keep track of it all without his help.\n\nDoctor: Have these memory and thinking problems been steadily getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it's steadily getting worse, to be honest. It's... well, it's hard to judge. I haven't really kept track of whether it's a little worse today than it was last month, or if it's just been the same amount of foggy this whole time. I just know that right now, it's a real problem and it's very frustrating.\n\nDoctor: Has a doctor told you that these memory problems are due to a medical condition affecting your brain or body?\n\nPatient: No, no doctor has ever said anything like that to me. I haven't been told that it's from a medical condition or anything wrong with my brain or body. I just know my memory isn't what it used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling very anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I get frustrated when I forget things, but I wouldn't say I've been feeling overly anxious or worried about everything. Just... normal, I suppose, other than the memory stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever gotten lost in a place that used to be familiar to you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I might forget where I put my keys, but I always know where I am. I haven't ever gotten lost in my own neighborhood or anywhere I know well.\n\nDoctor: Has any doctor ever told you that a medical illness or brain condition is causing your memory problems?\n\nPatient: I think you just asked me that one a minute ago. But no... like I said, no doctor has ever told me it was from a medical illness or a brain condition. Nobody has ever said anything like that to me.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you noticed problems with your thinking or judgment, not just your memory?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. My family hasn't said anything about my judgment or my thinking. It's really just the memory things that come up.\n\nDoctor: Compared with how you used to be, has your memory clearly gotten worse?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say it's clearly gotten worse. I mean, I'm getting older, so I might have those little moments like with the keys, but... no, there hasn't been any big change or anything like that. It seems about the same to me.\n\nDoctor: Have your memory problems caused you to stop doing important activities or responsibilities you used to handle on your own?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I guess they have. I’ve missed some doctor visits recently, and I tangled up the schedule at the senior center so badly that my husband had to step in. He’s been helping me with a lot of it now—making the phone calls, keeping the calendars straight, and even handling repair decisions around the house that I always used to just take care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Has any doctor ever diagnosed you with dementia or a neurocognitive disorder?\n\nPatient: No, no doctor has ever told me I have dementia. Or... whatever that other word was you used. I've never been diagnosed with anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you noticed that you repeat the same questions or stories because you forget you already said them?\n\nPatient: No, my husband hasn't mentioned anything like that. We talk quite a bit, and I don't think I'm repeating myself or asking the same things over and over. If I was, I'm sure he'd let me know.\n\nDoctor: Has your husband noticed that your memory has been getting worse?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, he stepped in to help with the calendar and the phone calls, like I mentioned, but he hasn't actually said anything about my memory getting worse. If he's noticed something like that, he hasn't mentioned it to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any memory or thinking tests done by a doctor that showed a problem?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any tests like that done. My regular doctor has never asked me to take a memory test or anything, so there hasn't been anything to show a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a memory test or thinking test that showed your memory was actually impaired?\n\nPatient: Like I just mentioned a moment ago, no. I've never taken any kind of memory test, so there hasn't been anything to show it's impaired.\n\nDoctor: Has your husband clearly noticed that your memory is worse than it used to be?\n\nPatient: No, like I was saying, he hasn't said anything like that to me. He helps out more with the schedule and things, but he's never actually told me my memory is worse than it used to be.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I haven't been told that it's from a medical condition or anything wrong with my brain or body. I've never been diagnosed with anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you noticed that you repeat the same questions or stories because you forget you already said them?\n\nPatient: No, my husband hasn't mentioned anything like that.
